Next, we will show that system (2.5) has a global attractor. By using the similar method as in Martcheva and Thieme (2003) and Cheng et al. (2018), we can state the following result.

Lemma 5.4

There exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {A}}_{0}$$\end{document}A0, a compact subset of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {Y}}_+$$\end{document}Y+, which is a global attractor for the solution semiflow of system (2.5). Moreover, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {A}}_{0}$$\end{document}A0 is invariant under the solution semiflow, that is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \varvec{\Psi }^*(t,\varvec{\varphi }_{0})\subseteq {\mathcal {A}}_{0},\quad \text{ for } \text{ every }\quad \varvec{\varphi }_{0}\in {\mathcal {A}}_{0},\quad \forall t\ge 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Ψ∗(t,φ0)⊆A0,foreveryφ0∈A0,∀t≥0.

Proof

We show that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varvec{\Psi }^*$$\end{document}Ψ∗ satisfies the assumptions of Lemma 3.2.3 and Theorem 3.4.6 in Hale (2010). To this end, we split the solution semiflow into two parts. For any initial condition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varvec{\varphi }_{0}\in {\mathcal {Y}}_+$$\end{document}φ0∈Y+, we let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varvec{\Psi }^*(t,\varvec{\varphi }_{0})=\widehat{\varvec{\Psi }}^*(t,\varvec{\varphi }_{0})+ \widetilde{\varvec{\Psi }}^*(t,\varvec{\varphi }_{0})$$\end{document}Ψ∗(t,φ0)=Ψ^∗(t,φ0)+Ψ~∗(t,φ0), where\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \widehat{\varvec{\Psi }}^*(t,\varvec{\varphi }_{0})=\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} \displaystyle ({{\textbf {0}}}_{{\mathbb {R}}^n},{{\textbf {0}}}_{L^1}), &{} t>\tau , \\ \displaystyle ({{\textbf {0}}}_{{\mathbb {R}}^n},{{\textbf {I}}}(t,\tau )), &{} t\le \tau , \end{array} \right. \quad \text{ and }\quad \widetilde{\varvec{\Psi }}^*(t,\varvec{\varphi }_{0})=\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} \displaystyle ({{\textbf {S}}}(t),{{\textbf {I}}}(t,\tau )), &{} t>\tau , \\ \displaystyle ({{\textbf {S}}}(t),{{\textbf {0}}}_{L^1}), &{} t\le \tau . \end{array} \right. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Ψ^∗(t,φ0)=(0Rn,0L1),t>τ,(0Rn,I(t,τ)),t≤τ,andΨ~∗(t,φ0)=(S(t),I(t,τ)),t>τ,(S(t),0L1),t≤τ.In such a way, we need to prove the following claim:

Claim 5.5

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\varvec{\Psi }}^*(t,\varvec{\varphi }_{0})\rightarrow 0$$\end{document}Ψ^∗(t,φ0)→0 as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t\rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}t→∞ for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varvec{\varphi }_{0}$$\end{document}φ0 in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {Y}}$$\end{document}Y.For a fixed t and any bounded set B in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {Y}}$$\end{document}Y, the set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{\widetilde{\varvec{\Psi }}^*(t,\varvec{\varphi }_{0})\;:\; \varvec{\varphi }_{0}\in B\}$$\end{document}{Ψ~∗(t,φ0):φ0∈B} is precompact.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\varvec{\Psi }}^*(t,\varvec{\varphi }_{0})\rightarrow 0$$\end{document}Ψ^∗(t,φ0)→0 as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t\rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}t→∞ for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varvec{\varphi }_{0}$$\end{document}φ0 in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {Y}}$$\end{document}Y.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{\varvec{\Psi }}^*(t,\varvec{\varphi }_{0})\rightarrow 0$$\end{document}Ψ^∗(t,φ0)→0 as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t\rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}t→∞ for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varvec{\varphi }_{0}$$\end{document}φ0 in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {Y}}$$\end{document}Y.

For a fixed t and any bounded set B in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {Y}}$$\end{document}Y, the set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{\widetilde{\varvec{\Psi }}^*(t,\varvec{\varphi }_{0})\;:\; \varvec{\varphi }_{0}\in B\}$$\end{document}{Ψ~∗(t,φ0):φ0∈B} is precompact.

For a fixed t and any bounded set B in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {Y}}$$\end{document}Y, the set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{\widetilde{\varvec{\Psi }}^*(t,\varvec{\varphi }_{0})\;:\; \varvec{\varphi }_{0}\in B\}$$\end{document}{Ψ~∗(t,φ0):φ0∈B} is precompact.

Proof of Claim 5.5

Therefore, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lim \limits _{t\rightarrow \infty }\Vert \widetilde{{{\textbf {I}}}}_i^n(t,\cdot )\Vert _{L^1}=+\infty $$\end{document}limt→∞‖I~in(t,·)‖L1=+∞ and from (5.21), we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lim \limits _{t\rightarrow \infty }\Vert {{\textbf {I}}}_i^n(t,\cdot )\Vert _{L^1}=+\infty $$\end{document}limt→∞‖Iin(t,·)‖L1=+∞, which is a contradiction to the boundedness of the solution. Thus, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W^S(\{{{\textbf {E}}}^0\})\cap {\mathcal {M}}=\emptyset $$\end{document}WS({E0})∩M=∅ and we derive from Theorem 4.2 in Hale and Waltman (1989) that the semiflow \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{\varvec{\Psi }(t,\cdot )\}_{t\ge 0}$$\end{document}{Ψ(t,·)}t≥0 is uniform persistent with respect to the pair \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$({\mathcal {M}},\partial {\mathcal {M}})$$\end{document}(M,∂M). Moreover, by Theorem 3.7 in Magal and Zhao (2005), there exists a compact global attractor \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {A}}_1\subset {\mathcal {M}}$$\end{document}A1⊂M for the semiflow \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{\varvec{\Psi }(t,\cdot )\}_{t\ge 0}$$\end{document}{Ψ(t,·)}t≥0. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Proof of Theorem 2.7

The proof of Theorem 2.7 is decomposed into two parts. The first part is devoted to the global stability of the disease-free equilibrium, while the second part is devoted to the global stability of the endemic equilibrium.

Proof of Theorem 2.7 (i): global stability of the disease-free equilibrium

When the initial condition of System (2.5) satisfies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varvec{\varphi }_{0}\in \partial {\mathcal {M}}$$\end{document}φ0∈∂M, i.e., \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum _{i\in {\mathcal {I}}}\int _0^{\infty } \left\langle \varvec{\beta }_i(\tau ),{{\textbf {I}}}_{i0}(\tau ) \right\rangle \textrm{d}\tau =0$$\end{document}∑i∈I∫0∞βi(τ),Ii0(τ)dτ=0, by Claim 5.7, it comes that the semiflow \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varvec{\Psi }(t,\varvec{\varphi }_{0})$$\end{document}Ψ(t,φ0) generated by system (2.5) is such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lim \limits _{t\rightarrow \infty }\varvec{\Psi }(t,\varvec{\varphi }_{0})=({{\textbf {S}}}^0,{{\textbf {0}}}_{{\mathbb {R}}^{2n}},{{\textbf {0}}}_{L_+^1((0,\infty ),{\mathbb {R}}^{2n})})$$\end{document}limt→∞Ψ(t,φ0)=(S0,0R2n,0L+1((0,∞),R2n)). It then remain to prove the global stability of the disease-free equilibrium when \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {R}}_0\le 1$$\end{document}R0≤1.

Theorem 6.1

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {R}}_0\le 1$$\end{document}R0≤1 then, the disease-free equilibrium \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\textbf {E}}}^0=({{\textbf {S}}}^0,\,{{\textbf {0}}}_{L^1((0,\infty )\times {\mathbb {R}},{\mathbb {R}}^{2n})})$$\end{document}E0=(S0,0L1((0,∞)×R,R2n)) of system (2.5) is globally asymptotically stable.

It is easy to verify that largest invariant set in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ \frac{dV}{dt}=0\right\} $$\end{document}dVdt=0 is the singleton \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{{{\textbf {E}}}^0\}$$\end{document}{E0}. Thus, all solutions of system (2.5) converge to the disease-free equilibrium \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\textbf {E}}}^0$$\end{document}E0. Hence, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${{\textbf {E}}}^0$$\end{document}E0 is globally asymptotically stable when \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\mathcal {R}}_0\le 1$$\end{document}R0≤1. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Proof of Theorem 2.7 (ii): global stability of the endemic equilibrium
